EXPERIMENTAL 
Submicron-sized (average particle size; 0.3 m), micron-sized (2.3 m) or coarse -SiC 
powder (5.3 m) was used as the starting materials. Figure 2 shows SEM micrographs of -SiC 
powder used as the starting materials. In this study, Al-B-C system was selected as the sintering 
additives and grain growth promotors. Al-B-C system has been known as the sintering additives 
for SiC.12,13 As compared with the reports on the densification of SiC using Al-B-C sintering 
additives, the present amount of the sintering additives was extremely low in order to form 
porous structure in SiC ceramics.12,13 Cao et al. fabricated dense SiC ceramics using -SiC 
powder and Al-B-C additives (3 wt% Al - 0.6 wt% B - 2 wt% C) by hot-pressing, and plate-like 
grains were grown in the dense SiC ceramics.13 The compositions of B (Rare Metallic Co., Ltd., 
Japan) and C (Asahi Thermal, Asahi Carbon Co., Ltd., Japan) were 0.15 wt% and 1.00 wt%, 
respectively. Aluminum nitrate enneahydrate (Al(NO3)3•9H2O; Nacalai Tesque, Inc., Japan) was 
used as the Al source, and the composition of Al was adjusted to 0.1 wt%. Beta-SiC powder and 
sintering additives were mixed by wet ball-milling for 24 h using SiC balls, and then dried with a 
rotary evaporator. The dried powder was crushed with SiC mortar and pestle, followed by 
176
·
Advances in Bioceramics and Porous Ceramics VII
Effects of SiC Particle Size and Sintering Temperature on Microstructure
sieving with mesh with opening of 300 
m. Spherical polymethyl methacrylate (PMMA) 
particles (MR-30, average particle size; 30 m, Soken Chemical & Engineering Co., Ltd., Japan) 
were used as pore formers to fabricate porous SiC ceramics. PMMA particles were added to the 
starting powders. The amount of PMMA particles was 17 wt% (40 vol%). The powder was 
mixed with SiC mortar and pestle. After mixing, the powder was sieved through mesh with 
opening of 300 m. The powder was formed into a cylindrical compact by uniaxial pressing at 
17 MPa, followed by heat-treatment at 400oC for 1h in air at a heating rate of 1oC/min in order to 
remove the PMMA particles in the compacts. The compacts were pressureless-sintered at 
1850-2150oC for 30 min in argon atmosphere at a heating rate of 30oC/min. The compacts were 
heated under vacuum from room temperature to 1500oC. After being held at 1500oC under 
vacuum for 30 min, argon gas was fed into the furnace, and the compacts were heated to the 
desired temperature in argon flow. Bulk density and open porosity of the porous SiC ceramics 
were measured by Archimedes’ method. Their microstructure was observed with a field emission 
scanning electron microscope (FE-SEM; S-4800, Hitachi, Ltd., Japan). Crystalline phases of the 
porous SiC ceramics were identified by a powder X-ray diffraction analysis (PW-1700, Philips, 
the Netherlands) and the distribution of SiC polytypes in the porous SiC ceramics was 
determined by Ruska’s method.14 
 
RESULTS 
 
Porous SiC Ceramics using Submicron-Sized -SiC Powder (0.3 m) 
Figure 3 shows SEM micrographs of the porous SiC ceramics using submicron-sized -SiC 
powder sintered at 1850oC-2150oC. At 1850oC and 1950oC, grain growth did not occur during 
sintering, and plate-like SiC grains were not observed. At 2050oC, grain growth was promoted 
and the formation of plate-like grains started. At 2150oC, the porous SiC ceramics consisted of 
plate-like SiC grains of around 10-20 m, and the plate-like SiC grains stuck out of the walls of 
pores and the sintered surface in the porous SiC ceramics. Bulk density and open porosity of the 
porous SiC ceramics sintered at 2150oC were 1.43 g/cm3 and 52.0 %, respectively. Their volume 
shrinkage after sintering at 2150oC was around 5 %. 
Fractions of SiC polytypes in the porous SiC ceramics using the submicron-sized -SiC 
powder sintered at 1850oC-2150oC are listed in Table I. The porous SiC ceramics sintered at 
1850oC and 1950oC mainly consisted of 3C ( -SiC), and the fraction of 3C in the porous SiC 
ceramics was around 90 %. The fraction of 3C decreased and the formation of 6H ( -SiC) was 
Advances in Bioceramics and Porous Ceramics VII
·
177
Effects of SiC Particle Size and Sintering Temperature on Microstructure
enhanced with increasing sintering temperature, and the fraction of 6H in the porous SiC 
ceramics sintered at 2150oC was 76 %. In addition, the fraction of 4H ( -SiC) also increased 
from 1 % to 8 %. From this result, it is suggested that the plate-like SiC grains formed in the 
porous SiC ceramics during sintering mainly corresponded to 6H phase. 
 
Porous SiC Ceramics using Micron-Sized -SiC Powder (2.3 m) 
Figure 4 exhibits SEM micrographs of the porous SiC ceramics using micron-sized -SiC 
powder sintered at 2000oC and 2150oC. Whereas SiC grains were not grown in porous SiC 
ceramics sintered at 2000oC, large SiC grains were observed in porous SiC ceramics sintered at 
2150oC. Large plate-like SiC grains of about 30-40 m in maximum grew during sintering and 
the porous SiC ceramics consisted of entangled large plate-like SiC grains. In addition, the 
number and the size of gaps among plate-like grains also increased with increasing the particle 
size of the starting -SiC powder. Bulk density of the porous SiC ceramics sintered at 2150oC 
was 1.13 g/cm3 and their open porosity was 65.7 %. The volume shrinkage of the porous SiC 
ceramics sintered at 2150oC was about 6 %. The porous SiC ceramics using the micron-sized 
-SiC powder showed higher open porosity than the porous SiC ceramics using the 
Figure 3. SEM micrographs of the porous SiC ceramics using submicron-sized -SiC 
powder (0.3 m) sintered at (a) 1850oC, (b) 1950oC, (c) 2050oC and (d) 2150oC.

Effects of SiC Particle Size and Sintering Temperature on Microstructure
submicron-sized -SiC powder due to the increase in the number and the size of gaps among 
plate-like grains and the pore size formed by pore formers. 
Fractions of SiC polytypes in the porous SiC ceramics using the micron-sized -SiC powder 
sintered at 2000oC and 2150oC are listed in Table I. At 2000oC, 3C phase predominantly existed 
in the porous SiC ceramics, and its fraction was around 90 %. In the case of the porous SiC 
ceramics sintered at 2150oC, 6H was major phase in porous SiC ceramics, and its fraction was 
around 80 %. This value was slightly higher than the porous SiC ceramics using the 
submicron-sized -SiC powder. Furthermore, the fractions of 4H also increased from 2 % to 15% 
as sintering temperature increased. The results suggested that the porous SiC ceramics mainly 
consisted of 6H and 4H phases. 
Kistler-De Coppi et al. investigated the phase transformation in SiC powders.15 6H phase was 
major phase in SiC powder with 0.1 wt% Al or 0.15 wt% B addition heated at 2150oC for 6 h in 
argon atmosphere. This result revealed that the formation of 6H phase would be promoted at the 
small amount of Al or B addition. The results obtained in this study also suggested that the small 
amount of Al and B additions would promote the formation of 6H phase at 2150oC. Our results 
were consistent with their results. 
                                                                                 
Porous SiC Ceramics using Coarse -SiC Powder (5.9 m) 
SEM micrograph of the SiC porous ceramics using coarse -SiC powder sintered at 2150oC 
is exhibited in Fig. 5. The porous SiC ceramics had a bulk density of 1.31 g/cm3 and an open 
porosity of 57.8 %. The volume shrinkage of the porous SiC ceramics was around 5 %. Despite 
sintering at 2150oC SiC grains did not grow and plate-like SiC grains were not observed in the 
porous SiC ceramics using the coarse -SiC powder. It seemed that sintering of the porous SiC

DISCUSSION 
 
In order to discuss the effect of Al-B-C addition to SiC on -  phase transformation of SiC 
and microstructure of porous SiC ceramics, porous SiC ceramics without Al-B-C additives were 
fabricated at 2150oC, and their microstructure and fractions of SiC polytypes in the porous SiC 
ceramics were evaluated. Figure 6 exhibits SEM micrographs of the porous SiC ceramics 
without Al-B-C additives sintered at 2150oC. In the case of submicron-sized -SiC powder, only 
a few SiC grains were grown into plate-like shape. As for the porous SiC ceramics using 
micron-sized and coarse -SiC powders, plate-like SiC grains were not grown without additives. 
Table I lists fractions of SiC polytypes in the porous SiC ceramics without Al-B-C additives. 
The fraction of 3C phase in the porous SiC ceramics without Al-B-C additives was over 70%, 
and this means that -  phase transformation and plate-like grain growth were well promoted at 
2150oC with Al-B-C addition. On the other hand, -  phase transformation did not take place in 
the porous SiC ceramics using the coarse -SiC powder, and the coarse -SiC particles did not 
grow into plate-like shapes at 2150oC in spite of Al-B-C addition. It was reported that the rate of 
Figure 6. SEM micrographs of the porous SiC ceramics using (a) Submicron-sized (0.3 m), 
(b) micron-sized (2.3 m) and (c) coarse -SiC powder (5.3 m) without Al-B-C additives 
sintered at 2150oC.  
Advances in Bioceramics and Porous Ceramics VII
·
181
Effects of SiC Particle Size and Sintering Temperature on Microstructure
-
phase transformation of SiC powder was proportional to the dimension of the specific 
surface area of the powder.15 The present result suggested that the size of plate-like SiC grains 
grown in the porous SiC ceramics depended on the starting -SiC particle size. However, the 
coarse -SiC particles did not transform into -phase easily and did not grow into the plate-like 
shape under the present sintering condition, and this result was consistent with their report. 
Furthermore, impurities in the starting SiC powders may affect the grain growth in the 
porous SiC ceramics because the amount of the additives was very small. Submicron-sized and 
coarse 
-SiC powders contain traces of iron, aluminum, and calcium as impurities, and 
micron-sized -SiC powder does not contain the metallic impurities because the -SiC powder 
was synthesized via liquid phase route. As mentioned above, only a few SiC grains were grown 
into plate-like shape in the porous SiC using submicron-sized -SiC powder without Al-B-C 
additives. At present, it has been unclear whether this grain growth occurred by the effect of 
-SiC particle size or impurities. In our future works, the effect of impurities in the starting SiC 
powders, notably the submicron-sized -SiC powder, on the grain growth in the porous SiC 
ceramics should be considered to clarify the in-situ growth mechanism in our porous SiC 
ceramics.
From the results, it is concluded that the porous SiC ceramics with a unique morphology of 
entangled large plate-like SiC grains were achieved using the micron-sized -SiC powder with 
small amount of Al and B additions at 2150oC, and the plate-like SiC grains formed in the porous 
SiC ceramics during sintering mainly corresponded to 6H. 
 
CONCLUSION 
Porous silicon carbide (SiC) ceramics were fabricated based on in-situ grain growth, and the 
effects of -SiC particle size and sintering temperature on the microstructure of the porous SiC 
ceramics were investigated. Submicron-sized (average particle size; 0.3 m), micron-sized (2.3 
m) or coarse -SiC powder (5.3 m) was uses as the starting material, and aluminum (Al), 
boron (B) and carbon (C) were added to SiC powder as sintering additives and grain growth 
promotors.  
In the case of the submicron-sized -SiC particles, grain growth was promoted and plate-like 
grains started to form at 2050oC, and the porous SiC ceramics consisted of plate-like SiC grains 
of around 10-20 m at 2150oC. Whereas SiC grains were not grown in porous SiC ceramics 
using the micron-sized -SiC particles sintered at 2000oC, large SiC grains were observed 
sintered at 2150oC. The porous SiC ceramics with a unique morphology of entangled large 
182
·
Advances in Bioceramics and Porous Ceramics VII
Effects of SiC Particle Size and Sintering Temperature on Microstructure
plate-like SiC grains of about 30-40 m in maximum were achieved using the micron-sized 
-SiC powder. XRD analysis suggested that the formation of 6H phase was promoted in the 
porous SiC ceramics using the submicron-sized or the micron-sized -SiC powder with small 
amount of Al and B additions at 2150oC, i.e. the small amount of Al and B additions would 
promote the formation of 6H phase in the porous SiC ceramics at 2150oC. In addition, the 
plate-like SiC grains mainly corresponded to 6H phase. On the other hand, 
-  phase 
transformation did not take place in the porous SiC ceramics using coarse -SiC powder, and the 
coarse -SiC particles did not grow into plate-like shapes at 2150oC in spite of Al-B-C addition.
